\begin{abstract}Every move below was produced by relational analogy to named master games (human and/or top-engine -- the exact source is named per move). Each figure shows our position with Melanie's move (a) and the top retrieved analog positions (b--d) with the master move played there; the caption names the exact source and the piece-role correspondence under $\mu$ -- cross-type maps (a rook playing a queen's role) are called out, as no material/eval engine explains a move that way.\end{abstract}

\caption{\textbf{Move 7, White: \texttt{Qg4}.} (a) Our position; Melanie plays \texttt{Qg4} (\texttt{d1g4}). (c) Retrieved from \texttt{lichess\_elite\_2024-09\_p0022\#195} (ply 20) [structural distance $d$=88, QAP obj=128]: the master played \texttt{dxc5}; under $\mu$ our \emph{Pd4} plays the role of that game's \emph{Pd4}; cross-type roles: our \emph{R}h1 as their \emph{B}c1, our \emph{B}c1 as their \emph{N}g1; transferred move \texttt{d4c5}. Synthesis: 1 retrieved analog transferred to \texttt{Qg4} (summed QAP objective 84, the strongest correspondence among the candidate moves); Melanie committed it via the \texttt{search} selector.}
